Let's consider using full barrier gradient with multiplicative (exponential) updates:
Update a_i ← a_i * exp(-η [1/S - G_i]) where G_i = 2 a_i ∑ q a_{k-i}. This is mirror descent with KL potential. For convex F, mirror descent with proper η can converge; relative step may be stable. Since F's Bregent potential in x? Actually multiplicative update corresponds gradient step in x: x_i -= η ∇F_i. If update a multiplied by exp(-η∇F_i), that's x step. Absolute η in log. We can choose η based on max gradient. Many previous mirror descent used normalized grad.

We can design an algorithm in x:
- Initialize a from parent scaled to cmax=1 (or cmax target).
- Iterate:
  - Compute c, S, f, F maybe.
  - Compute q_k = stable logistic(μ f_k) / c_k.
  - Compute h_i = ∑_k q_k a_{k-i} via Hankel FFT.
  - Gradient grad_i = -a_i/S + 2 a_i h_i.
  - Update x_i -= η grad_i (or a_i *= exp(-η grad_i)).

Maybe we can solve F_μ using accelerated gradient with 3-point gradient? Implement:
```
def objective_and_grad(x, mu):
    a=np.exp(x)
    S=a.sum()
    if S<=0: ...
    c=conv_self(a)
    f=np.log(np.maximum(c, 1e-300))
    # softplus stable:
    z=mu*f
    sp = log1p(exp(z)) (stable)
    val = -np.log(S) + np.sum(sp)/mu
    sig = 1/(1+exp(-z)) stable
    q = sig / np.maximum(c,eps)
    A=fft(a)
    Q=fft(q)
    h=real(ifft(A*conj(Q)))[:n]
    grad = -a/S + 2*a*h
    return val, grad
```

step = prev_step

Let's design a very good local optimizer for sum=1 using exact (or smoothed) subgradient, with line search, and initialize with one-sided power and parent. Let's design algorithms:
1. `point_convolution_weights`: Given a sum1, compute c, M. If M<=tiny return.
2. Weight selection methods:
   - broad: α_k = c_k / sum(c) = c_k (since sum c=1) All lags weighted by value. This is subgradient of average c? Gradient reduces total? It already makes v constant if current? Not.
   - smoothed: α_k = exp(β c_k)/sum, β=M? We can use β such that β M = t (e.g. 10-100). Since c values small, β maybe 50/M -> βM=50. But differences. Use stable: z=β*(c - M), exp.
   - topk: α over top k lags, uniform or weighted by c.
   - active equalization: α_k = max(0, c_k - τ)^p / sum, where τ = M - δ (select near max). This focuses on peaks but avoids single oscillation.
3. Compute v_i = H_α a (half derivative). Formula:
   - h = plus_correlation(a, α): h_i=∑_j a_j α_{i+j}, i=0..n-1. Implement via FFT circular correlation as before with FFT size >= L (2n-1). But α length L, a length n. For correlation with positive shift: ifft(FFT(a,N) * conj(FFT(α,N)))[:n]. Need ensure α padded length L. Good.
   - W_i = α_i + α_{i+1}+...+α_{i+n-1}: sliding window. Use cumulative: cum = np.concatenate([0, np.cumsum(α)]); W = cum[i+n]-cum[i] for i=0..n-1 (since L=2n-1, i+n-1 max 2n-2 for i=n-1, OK). Then v = 0.5*(a_i*W_i + h_i).
   Note: If α sum=1, W_i between0,1. v scale ~a. If α weights from c maybe sum1.
4. Update a:
   - Option reciprocal/log: `temp = a / (v + eps)**eta`, or `loga = log(a) - eta*(log(v+eps) - avg_log_v)`; then a=exp(loga), normalize to sum1. We need choose eta (0<eta<1).    - Or projective: a_new = project_simplex(a - step * (2v))? But reciprocal better. We can test both? Implement reciprocal with adaptive eta and exact objective line search: for a set of eta values (0.1,0.2,0.5) compute a_new, objective, pick best. If none improve, reduce eta.

Optimizer function `optimize_kkt(a, time, rng, beta_schedule)`:
- Maintain a sum1, positive floor.
- Track best_a, best_M.
- Main loop until time:
  - If time critical, break.
  - Compute c, M. If M improved, update best, maybe reset counters.
  - Choose weights α. We can cycle modes:
     * mode 0: broad active: α = smooth_top with threshold τ = M - tol*(M - minc) maybe top fraction. Use softmax β. Let β = peak_beta / M (so β M = peak_beta). Start βM=5, increase to 50 or 100. But if c differences small, β large. Use stable softmax over c. For α all positive, W/h cost same.
     * mode 1: top-k uniform: choose k gradually from 1 to Kmax (e.g., 64/128). Uniform over top k? Weighted by c? The derivative of max uses uniform over active face; if peaks exact equal, any. Uniform may be more stable. Weighted by c may overweight slightly higher peaks. Try both.
  - Compute v.
  - Candidate updates:
     1. Reciprocal: for eta in [0.2,0.5,1.0 maybe], `r = v / max(mean(v), tiny)`, `a_cand = a / r**eta` or `a * np.exp(-eta*(r-1))`; floor, normalize.
     2. Log KKT: `loga = np.log(a); t = np.log(v+eps); m = np.average(loga? no)`. Need target: make v constant. If we set log v_i → mean log v, update `x_new = x - eta*(log(v_i) - mean(log(v_i))) + shift_to_keep_sum` then a=exp(x_new) normalize. This is same as reciprocal with eta relative. The `a / v^eta` does not preserve mean of log v? It reduces high and increases low if η>0 (since 1/v). Good. Use `r=v/v_mean`; `new=a / r^η` (since low v r<1 => increase? Wait division by r^η: if r<1, denominator <1 => increase; if r>1 decrease. Correct.) Then normalize. This may overshoot if v distribution wide. Try η.
     3. Euclidean negative gradient: `g=2v`; step s=eta*S? `a_cand = project_simplex(a - step*g)` maybe with line search. But project may be heavy; can include small step.
  - For each candidate, compute objective M_cand (FFT conv). Select min. If min < current M - tol, accept; else try smaller eta; if no improvement, maybe perturb (add small noise) or switch to gradient descent.
- To avoid stagnation, if no improvement for many iters, switch weight mode, increase beta, or do a random smooth perturbation accepted if not too worse (or best keep). Also periodically do a short "exact gradient descent" with corrected gradient and backtracking.

Can we make line search over eta efficient? For reciprocal, computing candidate for η=0.2,0.5,1.0 each needs conv: 3 extra FFTs. Current conv already computed. Could be okay. def full_convolution(a):
   n=len(a); L=2*n-1; N=1 << (L-1).bit_length(); A=rfft(a,N); return irfft(A*A,N)[:L]
def correlation_plus(a, w):

```

Let's craft weight selection.

Softmax weights:
```
def softmax_lag_weights(c, hotness):  # hotness dimensionless, β = hotness/max(c)
    m = c.max()
    if m <=0: uniform
    z = (hotness/m)* (c - m)
    # stable exp
    e=np.exp(z); return e/e.sum()
```
As hotness increases, weights concentrate on lags with c close to m. If many equal, uniform over near max.
Top-k uniform:
```
def topk_uniform_weights(c, k):
   idx=np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
   w=np.zeros(len(c)); w[idx]=1/k; return w
```
Top-k weighted by c/sum top.
Threshold weights:
```
def threshold_weights(c, frac): # c >= m - frac*(m-minc)
   thresh = m - frac*(m - c.min())
   mask=c>=thresh
   if none mask top1
   w=c[mask]; w/=w.sum()
```

Line search for reciprocal:
```
def try_step(a, v, eta):
   r = v / max(v.mean(), tiny)
   # clip r to [1e-8,1e8]
   r = np.clip(r, 1e-8, 1e8)
   cand = a / (r ** eta)
   # If a has zeros, log floor. Use floor a_min=1e-15 before.
   cand = np.maximum(cand, a_floor)
   s=cand.sum()
   if s<=0: return None
   cand = cand / s
   return cand
```
We can instead use `np.exp(np.log(a_floor) - eta*(np.log(r)))` if a zero. Simpler keep a floor.

Let's outline `optimize_local(a0, time_limit, seed, mode_start=...)`:
Inputs `deadline` absolute.
- Initialize a = safe normalize.
- floor=1e-12? Need not too high: a average 1/n=1.2e-4. floor 1e-12 negligible. Use 1e-15.
- best_a=a; best_M=conv_max(a)
- eta=0.3; no_improve=0; total_iters=0.
- Weight parameters: hotness=10.0 maybe; top_k=1 or 16. We'll cycle.
- For time:
   - If time left < 5 break.
   - `c, M = conv_info(a)`
   - if M < best_M: update best, no_improve=0, maybe eta*=1.1
   - else no_improve++
   - Choose weights based on cycle:
      * If no_improve>2000, choose perturbed weights.
      * mode = it % 3:
         0: top-k with k cycling 1,2,4,8,16,32 (maybe weighted by c)
         1: softmax hotness cycling 5,10,20,50,100
         2: threshold fraction cycling 0.001,0.005,0.01,0.05,0.2
   - Compute v via `compute_v(a, alpha)`.
   - Try candidate reciprocal with eta. Evaluate M2. If M2 < M - 1e-14: accept, a=cand, eta=min(0.9, eta*1.1), no_improve=0, continue.
   - Else try eta*0.5, eta*0.25 until improvement. If found update eta=eta_new? 
   - If not, try linear candidate with steps 0.1,0.02 (project).
   - If still not, maybe do reciprocal with log clipped different or random.
   - If no accept for >5000, do a warm restart: take best_a, add random perturbation scale 1e-3, normalize, set a, eta=0.3, no_improve=0. But this may lose local progress; best preserved. Use after long stall.
- Return best_a, best_M.

Algorithm hot loop with KKT variance:
1. Compute c,M and weights α.
2. h=corr(a, α), mean, r=h-mean, hr=corr(r, α).

4. Evaluate max conv; accept best.

Let's design final optimizer with KKT variance as core, plus reciprocal fallback. This is novel and likely better.

Pseudo:
```
def optimize_kkt(a0, max_time, rng, initial_eta=0.2, verbose=True):
    a = safe_normalize(a0)
    best_a,best_M = a, conv_max(a)
    step = 0.25
    hotness = 10; topk_cycle = 1; fraction_cycle=...
    stall=0; it=0
    deadline = now+max_time
    while now < deadline:
       c, M = convolution(a); # full c for weights
       if M < best_M - 1e-15:
           best_M=M; best_a=a; stall=0
           # increase step/hotness slightly
           step = min(0.45, step*1.05)
           hotness = min(200, hotness*1.02)
       else:
           stall +=1
       # choose alpha
       mode = it % 3
       if mode==0:
          k = min(topk_max, topk_base * (1 + stall//...)) but cycle [1,2,4,8,16,32,64]
          alpha = topk_uniform(c,k) or topk_weighted(c,k)
       elif mode==1:
          alpha = softmax_lag_weights(c, hotness)
       else:
          alpha = threshold_lag_weights(c, frac_cycle)
       # KKT variance steps
       accepted=False
       h = corr(a, alpha); mean = h.mean(); r=h-mean; hr=corr(r,alpha)
       for st in (step, step*0.5, step*2.0 clipped >0, 0.1):
           cand = a - st*hr
           cand = np.maximum(cand, floor)
           cand /= cand.sum()
           M2 = conv_max(cand)
           if M2 < M - 1e-14:
               a=cand; step = min(0.45, st*1.05) maybe; accepted=True; break
       if not accepted:
           # reciprocal step
           r = h / max(h.mean, floor); cand = a / r**eta ... evaluate
       if not accepted:
           # direct subgradient
           g=2*h; cand=project_simplex(a - sg*g); evaluate steps
       if not accepted:
           # if stall large, perturb best
           if stall > 2000:
               a = best_a + small noise; normalize
               step=0.15; hotness=5
           else:
               # accept a small random step? Maybe do nothing and adjust mode quickly
               pass
       it+=1
    return best
```
Need careful: If we don't accept, `a` unchanged; next iteration same c/alpha maybe. To avoid infinite loop, cycle mode/parameters quickly (e.g., mode = it%3 but if stuck α same, may keep failing). We should after failure update `step` down or use different alpha (e.g., next mode) even without acceptance. The loop increments it, mode changes, so alpha changes. Good.

  h=corr, r, hr

```
This might improve more per weight selection. But uses candidate conv each step. We can combine with exact M evaluation. Let's implement a `rollout_kkt` function inside optimize but top-level? No closures; can be top-level taking parameters.
```
def kkt_rollout(a, alpha, step, max_steps, current_M):
   best_a=a; best_M=current_M
   for s in range(max_steps):
      h=corr_plus(a, alpha); mean...
      if max(abs(r)) small break
      hr=corr_plus(r, alpha)
      cand=normalize(a - step*hr)
      M2=conv_max(cand)
      if M2 < best_M - 1e-15:
          best_a=cand; best_M=M2
      # if M2 much worse than current, stop
      if M2 > current_M + 1e-5: break
      a=cand
   return best_a,best_M
```
Then outer chooses α, runs rollout with steps maybe 3-8. If best_M improves, accept (a=best_a). If not, try other step (smaller) or reciprocal. This may be more effective.

But if best_a from intermediate not the last, setting a=best_a and then recompute next. Good.

Now, there is another possible exact improvement: Since objective is scale invariant, we can after any candidate normalize sum to 1. def upsample_sequence(a, m):
   n=len(a)
   nnew=n*m
   y=np.repeat(a, m)
   # smooth with box kernel of size 3? length m? Use gaussian maybe.
   k=max(1, m//2)
   kernel=np.ones(k+1); kernel/=kernel.sum()
   y=np.convolve(y, kernel, mode='same')
   y=np.maximum(y,1e-15); y/=y.sum()
   return y
```

Let's code the optimizer in detail.

```
def optimize_kkt(a0, max_seconds, seed, verbose=True, eta_recip=0.3):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+max_seconds
    a=a0.copy(); floor=1e-15
    n=len(a)
    L=2*n-1
    N=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
    best_a=positive_normalize(a, floor)
    c_best, M_best = conv_full_fft(best_a)
    step_kkt=0.25
    step_lin=0.1
    hotness=10.0
    stall=0; it=0
    last_print=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 1:
       # compute c, M for current a
       c=conv_full_fft(a)
       M=c.max()
       if M < M_best - 1e-15:
          M_best=M; best_a=a.copy(); stall=0
          step_kkt=min(0.45, step_kkt*1.05)
       else:
          stall +=1
       # if long stall, perturb or change params
       if stall > 500:
          # switch to broad weights and maybe reduce step
          step_kkt=max(0.1, step_kkt*0.7); hotness=5.0; stall=0? maybe
       # choose alpha
       if it % 4 == 0:
          # top-k weighted cycle
          k = 1 << ((it//100) % 8)  # 1,2,4,...128
          k = min(512, max(1,k))
          alpha = topk_weighted(c,k)
       elif it %4==1:
          hotness = 5 + 20*((it//100)%6)
          alpha = softmax_weights(c, hotness)
       elif it %4==2:
          frac = 0.001 * ((it//100)%8 +1)
          alpha = threshold_weights(c, min(0.5, frac))
       else:
          # reciprocal focused: top 1% uniform
          k=max(1,int(0.01*L))
          k=min(256,k)
          alpha=topk_uniform(c,k)
       # KKT rollout with current step and half step
       for f in (step_kkt, step_kkt*0.5):
          a2, M2 = kkt_rollout(a, alpha, f, max_steps=3, current_M=M, N=N)
          if M2 < M - 1e-15:
             a=a2.copy(); M=M2
             step_kkt = min(0.45, f*1.05)
             stall=0
             break
       else:
          # reciprocal candidate
          h = corr_plus(a, alpha, N)
          hm = h.mean()
          if hm > 1e-300:
             for eta in (eta_recip, eta_recip*0.5):
                cand = reciprocal_from_a_h(a,h,eta,floor)
                M2=conv_max_fft(cand,N)
                if M2 < M - 1e-15:
                   a=cand; M=M2; eta_recip=min(0.8, eta*1.1); stall=0; break
       if time.monotonic() < last_print + 30:
          pass
       else:
          print(...)
          last_print=time.monotonic()
       it +=1
    return best_a, M_best
```
Need functions use N parameter to avoid recomputing.
```
def next_fft_size(n): L=2*n-1; return 1 << (L-1).bit_length()
def conv_full_fft(a,N): A=rfft(a,N); c=irfft(A*A,N); L=len(a)*2-1; return c[:L]
def conv_max_fft(a,N): c=conv_full_fft(a,N); return c.max()
def corr_plus(a, alpha, N): A=rfft(a,N); B=rfft(alpha,N); return irfft(A*np.conj(B),N)[:len(a)]
```
In `kkt_rollout`, to compute candidate f, for each step maybe we need h and hr. But if f small, hr changes with a. Implementation:
```
def kkt_rollout(a, alpha, step, max_steps, current_M, N):
   best_a=a; best_M=current_M; a_curr=a; n=len(a); floor=...
   B = rfft(alpha,N)
   for s in range(max_steps):
      A=rfft(a_curr,N)
      h=irfft(A*np.conj(B),N)[:n]
      # if h all equal within tol break
      r=h - h.mean()
      if np.max(np.abs(r)) < 1e-12 * max(1.0, h.mean()): break
      R=rfft(r,N)
      hr=irfft(R*np.conj(B),N)[:n]
      cand = a_curr - step*hr
      cand = positive_normalize(cand, floor)
      M2 = conv_max_fft(cand,N)
      if M2 < best_M - 1e-15:
          best_a=cand; best_M=M2
      # if M2 not worse than current_M*1.000? continue, else break
      if M2 > current_M + 1e-8:
          break
      a_curr = cand
   return best_a,best_M
```
Should we update a_curr to cand even if M2 > best_M but <=current_M to continue? The direction based on current weights; multiple steps may monotone in variance but M might increase. Could continue if M2 < current_M (overall improves from original) even if not best? If M2 < M_original, set a_curr=cand and continue; best already. Else break to avoid bad. Good.
But note `current_M` passed is original; if first step improves M_original, we continue. If second step not best but still < original, continue? Might find best. OK.

Let's specify `conv_full_fft`:
```
def conv_full_fft(a, N=None):
    n=len(a)
    L=2*n-1
    if N is None:
       N=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
    A=rfft(a, N)
    c=irfft(A*A, N)
    c[:L] = np.maximum(c[:L], 0.0)
    return c[:L]
```
But assigning `c[:L]=...` makes copy? OK. `irfft` returns array length N. We can `c = np.maximum(c[:L].copy(), 0.0)`. Better.
`conv_max_fft`: similar but `return float(np.max(c))`.

`corr_plus`:
```
def corr_plus(a, alpha, N):
    n=len(a)
    A = rfft(a,N)
    B = rfft(alpha,N)
    h = irfft(A * np.conj(B), N)
    h = h[:n].copy()
    # remove tiny negative? h should nonnegative since a,alpha nonnegative, but roundoff
    h = np.maximum(h,0.0)
    return h
```
Since a,alpha nonnegative, h nonnegative; clip.

`corr_from_A_B(a, alpha_fft, N, n)`:
```
def corr_from_A_B(a, alpha_fft, N, n):
    A = rfft(a,N)
    h=irfft(A*np.conj(alpha_fft),N)[:n]
    return np.maximum(h,0.0)
```

In `kkt_rollout`, pass alpha_fft to avoid recomputing B. But need alpha maybe for? No.
```
def kkt_rollout(a0, alpha_fft, step, max_steps, current_M, N, floor=1e-12):
    n=len(a0)
    a=a0.copy(); best_a=a; best_M=current_M
    for _ in range(max_steps):
       A=rfft(a,N)
       h=irfft(A*np.conj(alpha_fft), N)[:n]
       h=np.maximum(h,0.0)
       mean = h.mean()
       if mean <= 1e-300: break
       r = h - mean
       # if variation tiny relative, break
       if np.max(np.abs(r)) < 1e-14 * max(1.0, mean): break
       R = rfft(r,N)
       hr=irfft(R*np.conj(alpha_fft),N)[:n]
       hr = np.wherenp??
       cand = a - step*hr
       cand = positive_normalize(cand, floor)
       M2 = conv_max_fft(cand,N)
       if M2 < best_M - 1e-15:
          best_a = cand.copy(); best_M = M2
       if M2 <= current_M + 1e-14:
          a = cand
       else:
          break
    return best_a, best_M
```
Need clip hr? H r can be negative/positive; no clip. positive_normalize cand.
Tolerance: M values ~1e-4. improvement 1e-15 too small? Use `1e-16` maybe. Due FFT noise, improvements <1e-15 might false. Use tolerance `eps_tol = 1e-12` absolute? Objective M ~1e-4, relative 1e-12 =1e-16, too strict. FFT error maybe 1e-19? Use `1e-15` OK. But if no improvement, stop. For phase improvements, target difference 1e-7. Use `improve_tol = 1e-15` fine.
When evaluating candidate M, convolution of cand normalized; if roundoff makes M slightly lower by 1e-16, accept; harmless. Could oscillate. Use `1e-13`? M=1e-4, 1e-13 is 0.1%? Actually 1e-13/1e-4=1e-9. Fine. Use `1e-13` for acceptance to avoid noise. Objective difference 1e-13 for M corresponds objective 2n*1e-13 ~0.0016 for n8192? Too large. Need smaller. Use `1e-15` => object diff 1.6e-11. Good. FFT error maybe <1e-16? Use 1e-14? object diff0.00016. OK target improvement 0.006. Use `accept_tol=1e-15` absolute M. def optimize_kkt(a0, max_time, seed=0, verbose=True):
   rng=...
   deadline=...
   a=positive_normalize(a0)
   n=len(a); N=next_fft_size(n); floor=1e-12
   c=conv_full_fft(a,N); M=c.max()
   best_a=a; best_M=M
   step=0.25; recip_eta=0.3; hotness=10
   stall=0; it=0; last_print=time.monotonic()
   while time.monotonic() < deadline-2:
      if time.monotonic() - last_print > 20: print...
      # weights based on it/stall
      if stall > 1000:
         # force broad top 5% and lower step
         ...
      mode = it % 4
      if mode==0: k=...; alpha=topk_weight
      elif mode==1: alpha=softmax...
      elif mode==2: alpha=threshold...
      else: alpha=topk_uniform...
      B=rfft(alpha,N)
      # KKT candidate
      cand1 = a
      cand1, M1 = kkt_rollout(a, B, step, 2, M, N, floor)
      if M1 < M - ACCEPT_TOL:
          a = cand1; M = M1; step = min(0.45, step*1.05); stall=0; it+=1; continue
      # reciprocal
      A=rfft(a,N); h=irfft(A*np.conj(B),N)[:n]; h=max
      hm=h.mean()
      if hm > 1e-300:
         for eta in (recip_eta, max(0.05, recip_eta*0.4)):
            r = h/hm
            r = np.clip(r, 1e-6, 1e6)
            cand = a * np.exp(-eta * np.log(r))  # or a / np.power(r, eta)
            cand=positive_normalize(cand)
            M2=conv_max_fft(cand,N)
            if M2 < M - ACCEPT_TOL:
                a=cand; M=M2; recip_eta=min(0.8, eta*1.1); stall=0; break
         else:
            # project gradient fallback
            g=2*h
            for sg in (0.1,0.02):
               cand=project_simplex(a - sg*g, 1.0)
               M2=conv_max_fft(cand,N)
               if M2 < M - ACCEPT_TOL:
                   a=cand; M=M2; sg=...; stall=0; break
      # if no accept, adjust
      if M < best_M - ACCEPT_TOL:
          best_M=M; best_a=a.copy(); stall=0
      else:
          stall +=1
          step = max(0.05, step*0.8)
          # if stalled heavily, perturb
          if stall % 500 == 499:
             noise = rng.normal(0, 1e-4, n)
             a=positive_normalize(best_a + noise)
             step=0.15; it=0? keep
      it +=1
    return best_a,best_M
```

Upsampling function: For m=2, use `np.interp`? Let's implement a smoother spectral-ish:
```
def upsample_sequence(a, m):
    n=len(a); nnew=n*m
    old = np.linspace(0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
    new = np.linspace(0, 1.0, nnew, endpoint=False)
    # periodic interpolation? Use np.interp with left/right edge values
    y = np.interp(new, old, a, left=a[0], right=a[-1])
    # smooth with small box
    k = max(3, m)
    kern = np.ones(k); kern/=k
    y = np.convolve(y, kern, mode='same')
    y = positive_normalize(y)
    return y
```
But parent not periodic; interpolation over [0,1] endpoint values. Good. For parent one-sided shape, upsample works. Use box smoothing to avoid oscillations. If m not integer? We only double.

For j≤n-1, k-j may be negative mod N = N + k -j. This is not zero if N+k-j < n? That would add wrap terms incorrectly? Example n=2, N=4, a=[a0,a1,0,0]. k=1: d_1= a0 a1 + a1 a0 + a2? In formula: j=0: a0 a1; j=1: a1 a0; j=2:0; j=3:0. No wrap because k-j for j>k: k=1,j? only 0,1. For k=2: j=1: a1 a1; j=0: a0 a2 (zero); no j=2 a2. Good. For linear conv length L<N, no wrap because second index k-j if negative is ≥N - (n-1) maybe > n? Since max j=n-1, min k-j = -(n-1), mod N = N+n-1 ≥ L? N≥L=2n-1, so N+n-1 ≥3n-2 > n, zero. Thus safe.

`positive_normalize(a, floor=FLOOR)`:
```
def positive_normalize(a, floor=1e-12):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, floor)
    s = a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full_like(a, 1.0/a.size)
    return a / s
```
If a was shaped, OK.

`project_simplex` copy previous but no bug:
```
def project_simplex(v, S):
    v=np.asarray(v,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n=len(v)
    if n==0: return v
    w=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cumsum=np.cumsum(w)
    idx=np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=np.float64)
    theta_cand=(cumsum-S)/idx
    valid=theta_cand <= w
    if not np.any(valid): rho=n
    else: rho=int(np.max(np.where(valid)[0])+1)
    theta=(cumsum[rho-1]-S)/rho
    return np.maximum(v-theta,0.0)
```

Weight functions:
```
def uniform_weights(L): return np.full(L, 1.0/L)
def topk_uniform(c,k): ...
def topk_weighted(c,k): ...
def softmax_weights(c, hotness):
   M=float(c.max())
   if M <= 0 or not finite: uniform
   z=(hotness/M)*(c-M)
   e=np.exp(z - np.max(z)) # max z=0 but for safety
   s=e.sum()
   if s <=0: uniform
   return e/s
def threshold_weights(c, frac):
   M=c.max(); m=c.min(); range=M-m
   if range <= 1e-18: uniform
   thresh = M - frac*range
   mask = c >= thresh
   if not mask.any(): idx=np.argmax(c); mask[idx]=True
   w=np.zeros(c.shape); w[mask]=c[mask]
   s=w.sum(); if s<=0 uniform else w/s
```
For threshold, if frac small and multiple near max with equal c, mask includes equals. Good.

`choose_weights(c, it, stall, n_lags)`:

def choose_topk_weights(c, stage):
   t=stage % 8
   k = 1 << t
   if k > 512: k=512
   # maybe cycle quality
   if t < 4: return topk_uniform(c,k)
   else: return topk_weighted(c,k)
```

else: k = max(1,int(0.01*L)); k=min(256,k); alpha=topk_uniform

def kkt_rollout(a0, alpha_fft, step, max_steps, current_M, N, floor=1e-12):
    n = len(a0)
    a = a0.copy()
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_M = current_M
    loss_tol = max(1e-10, 1e-8 * current_M) # current_M 1e-4 ->1e-10? Wait 1e-8*1e-4=1e-12; use max(1e-9? Let's set 1e-10)
```
Maybe allow loss up to 1e-9 absolute. M=1e-4, relative1e-5. Objective diff ~1.6e-4? Too large? 1e-9*2n=1.6e-5, OK. Use `loss_tol = max(1e-12, 1e-6 * current_M)` =1e-10. objective diff 1.6e-6. Fine.
Implementation:
```
    for _ in range(max_steps):
        A = rfft(a,N)
        h = irfft(A*np.conj(alpha_fft), N)
        h = h[:n].copy()
        h = np.maximum(h,0.0)
        mean = float(h.mean())
        if mean <= 1e-300: break
        r = h - mean
        rmax = float(np.max(np.abs(r)))
        if rmax < 1e-14 * max(1.0, mean): break
        R = rfft(r,N)
        hr = irfft(R*np.conj(alpha_fft), N)[:n]
        cand = a - step * hr
        cand = positive_normalize(cand, floor)
        M2 = conv_max_fft(cand, N)
        if M2 < best_M - ACCEPT_TOL:
            best_a = cand.copy(); best_M = M2
        if M2 <= current_M + loss_tol:
            a = cand
        else:
            break
    return best_a, best_M
```
If `max_steps` large and H has negative eigen, M2 may oscillate; break if worse than original by loss_tol. Good.

def optimize_kkt(a0, max_time, seed=1, verbose=True):
    a=positive_normalize(a0)
    n=len(a); N=next_fft_size(n)
    deadline=time.monotonic()+max_time
    best_a=a.copy(); c=conv_full_fft(a,N); best_M=float(c.max()); M=best_M
    step=0.25; lin_step=0.1; recip_eta=0.25; hotness=10.0
    stall=0; it=0; last_print=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 3:
       if verbose and time.monotonic()-last_print>20:
          print(...)
          last_print=...
       # if current not best? a may be not best; but M from last? We need recompute if a changed.
       c=conv_full_fft(a,N); M=float(c.max())
       if M < best_M - ACCEPT_TOL:
           best_M=M; best_a=a.copy(); stall=0
           step=min(0.45, step*1.03)
       # choose alpha
       L=len(c)
       stage = it // 200
       mode = it & 3
       if mode == 0:
          k = min(512, 1 << (stage % 9))
          if stage % 2 == 0: alpha = topk_uniform(c,k)
          else: alpha=topk_weighted(c,k)
       elif mode == 1:
          hot = 5.0 + 15.0 * ((stage % 7) + 1) # 20-115
          alpha=softmax_weights(c, hot)
       elif mode == 2:
          frac = 0.0005 * ((stage % 12) + 1) # 0.0005-0.006
          alpha=threshold_weights(c, min(0.3, frac))
       else:
          k=max(1, min(512, int(L*0.005)))
          alpha=topk_weighted(c,k)
       B=rfft(alpha,N)
       improved=False
       # KKT step
       c1, M1 = kkt_rollout(a, B, step, 3, M, N, FLOOR)
       if M1 < M - ACCEPT_TOL:
          a=c1; M=M1; step=min(0.45, step*1.03); improved=True
       if not improved:
          # try half KKT
          c2, M2 = kkt_rollout(a, B, max(0.05, step*0.5), 2, M, N, FLOOR)
          if M2 < M - ACCEPT_TOL: ...
       if not improved:
          # direct gradient using h from current a/B
          A=rfft(a,N); h=irfft(A*np.conj(B),N)[:n]; h=max
          for sg in (lin_step, max(0.02, lin_step*0.3)):
             cand=positive_normalize(a - sg*2.0*h)
             Mg=conv_max_fft(cand,N)
             if Mg < M - ACCEPT_TOL: a=cand; M=Mg; lin_step=...; improved=True; break
       if not improved:
          # reciprocal
          if h computed? We can compute if not. Let's compute h once before gradient/recip to reuse.
```

def analytic_init(n):

                for eta in (recip_eta, max(0.05, recip_eta*0.4)):

                    Mr=conv_max_fft(cand,N)
                    if ... recip_eta=...
```
After any improvement, at bottom update best if M<best.

    # recompute M? bottom update? Set M=conv_max

```python
"""
Search for non-negative sequences minimizing
    2*n*max(convo a,a) / (sum a)^2.

The code normalizes every candidate to sum 1, so the objective is 2*n*max_conv.
It uses FFT-based convolution/correlation and a KKT-inspired equilibration:
for a chosen active set of peak lags (weights alpha), define the Hankel
load H_alpha(a)_i = sum_j alpha_{i+j} a_j. At a first-order stationary point
for the active peaks this load is constant. The optimizer drives H_alpha(a)
toward constant with spectral matrix-vector products, while using exact
convolution maxima for monotone line search. Several peak-weightings and
fallback multiplicative/gradient updates are interleaved, with random
restarts and a final polish.
"""

import time
import numpy as np

try:
    from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft
except Exception:
    from numpy.fft import rfft, irfft

ACCEPT_TOL = 1e-15
FLOOR = 1e-12


def next_fft_size(n):
    L = 2 * n - 1
    return 1 << (L - 1).bit_length()


def positive_normalize(a, floor=FLOOR):
    """Project minimally to positive numbers and normalize sum to 1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, floor)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full(len(a), 1.0 / max(1, len(a)), dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


def project_simplex(v, S):
    """Euclidean projection onto {x >= 0, sum(x)=S}."""
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = len(v)
    if n == 0:
        return v
    w = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cumsum = np.cumsum(w)
    idx = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    theta_candidates = (cumsum - S) / idx
    valid = theta_candidates <= w
    if not np.any(valid):
        rho = n
    else:
        rho = int(np.max(np.where(valid)[0]) + 1)
    theta = (cumsum[rho - 1] - S) / rho
    return np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)


def conv_full_fft(a, N=None):
    """Full linear self-convolution of a, length 2n-1."""
    n = len(a)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    if N is None:
        N = next_fft_size(n)
    A = rfft(a, N)
    c = irfft(A * A, N)
    c = c[:L]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def conv_max_fft(a, N=None):
    """max(self-convolution(a)) for a positive array."""
    return float(np.max(conv_full_fft(a, N)))


def corr_plus(a, alpha, N):
    """
    h_i = sum_j a_j alpha_{i+j}, i=0..n-1.
    alpha length is 2n-1.
    """
    n = len(a)
    A = rfft(a, N)
    B = rfft(alpha, N)
    h = irfft(A * np.conj(B), N)
    h = h[:n]
    return np.maximum(h, 0.0).copy()


def topk_uniform(c, k):
    L = len(c)
    k = int(min(k, L))
    w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    if k <= 0:
        return w
    if k == 1:
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return w
    if k < L:
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
    else:
        idx = np.arange(L)
    w[idx] = 1.0 / k
    return w


def topk_weighted(c, k):
    L = len(c)
    k = int(min(k, L))
    w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    if k <= 0:
        return w
    if k == 1:
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return w
    if k < L:
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
    else:
        idx = np.arange(L)
    vals = c[idx].astype(np.float64)
    s = float(vals.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
        w[idx] = 1.0 / k
    else:
        w[idx] = vals / s
    return w


def softmax_lag_weights(c, hotness):
    """Weights proportional to exp(hotness*(c-max(c))/max(c))."""
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    M = float(np.max(c))
    L = len(c)
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0:
        return np.full(L, 1.0 / L, dtype=np.float64)
    z = (float(hotness) / M) * (c - M)
    z = np.where(np.isfinite(z), z, -700.0)
    z -= float(np.max(z))
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
        idx = int(np.argmax(c))
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[idx] = 1.0
        return w
    return e / s


def threshold_lag_weights(c, frac):
    """Normalize c over lags within frac of the full range from the max."""
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    L = len(c)
    M = float(np.max(c))
    m = float(np.min(c))
    rng = M - m
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0 or not np.isfinite(rng) or rng <= 1e-18:
        idx = int(np.argmax(c))
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[idx] = 1.0
        return w
    frac = float(min(max(frac, 1e-6), 0.999))
    thresh = M - frac * rng
    mask = c >= thresh
    if not np.any(mask):
        idx = int(np.argmax(c))
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[idx] = 1.0
        return w
    w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    w[mask] = c[mask]
    s = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
        w[mask] = 1.0
        s = float(w.sum())
    return w / s


def kkt_rollout(a0, alpha_fft, step, max_steps, current_M, N):
    """
    Several steepest descent steps for making H_alpha(a) constant, where
    alpha_fft is the rfft of the active weight vector.
    """
    n = len(a0)
    a = a0.copy()
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_M = float(current_M)
    loss_tol = max(1e-12, 1e-6 * max(current_M, 1e-12))

    for _ in range(int(max_steps)):
        A = rfft(a, N)
        h = irfft(A * np.conj(alpha_fft), N)
        h = np.maximum(h[:n].copy(), 0.0)
        mean_h = float(h.mean())
        if not np.isfinite(mean_h) or mean_h <= 1e-300:
            break
        r = h - mean_h
        rmax = float(np.max(np.abs(r)))
        if rmax < 1e-14 * max(1.0, mean_h):
            break
        R = rfft(r, N)
        hr = irfft(R * np.conj(alpha_fft), N)[:n]
        cand = a - float(step) * hr
        cand = positive_normalize(cand)
        M2 = conv_max_fft(cand, N)
        if not np.isfinite(M2):
            break
        if M2 < best_M - ACCEPT_TOL:
            best_a = cand.copy()
            best_M = float(M2)
        # Continue only while not materially worse than the starting point.
        if M2 <= float(current_M) + loss_tol:
            a = cand
        else:
            break
    return best_a, best_M


def analytic_init(n):
    """A useful one-sided boundary-singular starting shape."""
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a = 1.0 / np.sqrt(i + 1.0)
    return positive_normalize(a)


def upsample_sequence(a, factor=2):
    """Simple positive resampling to length len(a)*factor."""
    a = positive_normalize(a)
    n = len(a)
    nnew = int(n) * int(factor)
    if nnew <= n:
        return a.copy()
    old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
    new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, nnew, endpoint=False)
    y = np.interp(new, old, a, left=float(a[0]), right=float(a[-1]))
    k = max(3, int(factor) * 2 + 1)
    kernel = np.ones(k, dtype=np.float64)
    kernel /= kernel.sum()
    y = np.convolve(y, kernel, mode="same")
    return positive_normalize(y)


def optimize_kkt(a0, max_time, seed=1, verbose=True):
    """
    Main local optimizer. Returns (best_sequence, best_max_conv) for
    sequences normalized to sum 1.
    """
    a = positive_normalize(a0)
    n = len(a)
    if n < 4:
        c = conv_full_fft(a)
        return a, float(np.max(c))

    N = next_fft_size(n)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + float(max_time)

    c = conv_full_fft(a, N)
    M = float(np.max(c))
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_M = M

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed + 100)

    kkt_step = 0.25
    lin_step = 0.10
    recip_eta = 0.25
    stall = 0
    it = 0
    last_print = time.monotonic()

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 3.0:
        if verbose and (time.monotonic() - last_print > 25.0):
            obj = 2.0 * float(n) * best_M
            print("  kkt-opt it=%d best_obj=%.8f stall=%d kkt_step=%.4f" %
                  (it, obj, stall, kkt_step), flush=True)
            last_print = time.monotonic()

        c = conv_full_fft(a, N)
        M = float(np.max(c))

        if M < best_M - ACCEPT_TOL:
            best_M = M
            best_a = a.copy()
            stall = 0

        stage = it // 200
        mode = it & 3
        L = len(c)

        if mode == 0:
            k = min(512, 1 << (stage % 9))
            if stage % 2 == 0:
                alpha = topk_uniform(c, k)
            else:
                alpha = topk_weighted(c, k)
        elif mode == 1:
            hot = 5.0 + 15.0 * ((stage % 7) + 1)
            alpha = softmax_lag_weights(c, hot)
        elif mode == 2:
            frac = 0.0005 * ((stage % 12) + 1)
            alpha = threshold_lag_weights(c, min(0.30, frac))
        else:
            k = max(1, min(512, int(0.005 * L)))
            alpha = topk_weighted(c, k)

        B = rfft(alpha, N)
        improved = False

        # KKT equilibration on the selected active lag weights.
        cand, Mc = kkt_rollout(a, B, kkt_step, 3, M, N)
        if np.isfinite(Mc) and Mc < M - ACCEPT_TOL:
            a = cand
            M = float(Mc)
            kkt_step = min(0.45, kkt_step * 1.03)
            improved = True

        if not improved:
            cand, Mc = kkt_rollout(a, B, max(0.05, kkt_step * 0.5), 2, M, N)
            if np.isfinite(Mc) and Mc < M - ACCEPT_TOL:
                a = cand
                M = float(Mc)
                kkt_step = max(0.08, kkt_step * 0.9)
                improved = True

        if not improved:
            A = rfft(a, N)
            h = irfft(A * np.conj(B), N)
            h = np.maximum(h[:n].copy(), 0.0)

            # Direct subgradient step.
            for sg in (lin_step, max(0.02, lin_step * 0.35)):
                cand = positive_normalize(a - sg * (2.0 * h))
                Mg = conv_max_fft(cand, N)
                if np.isfinite(Mg) and Mg < M - ACCEPT_TOL:
                    a = cand
                    M = float(Mg)
                    lin_step = min(1.0, lin_step * 1.15)
                    improved = True
                    break

            # Multiplicative reciprocal-load update.
            hm = float(h.mean())
            if not improved and hm > 1e-300:
                rat = np.clip(h / hm, 1e-4, 1e4)
                for eta in (recip_eta, max(0.05, recip_eta * 0.4)):
                    cand = positive_normalize(a * np.exp(-eta * np.log(rat)))
                    Mr = conv_max_fft(cand, N)
                    if np.isfinite(Mr) and Mr < M - ACCEPT_TOL:
                        a = cand
                        M = float(Mr)
                        recip_eta = min(0.80, max(0.05, eta * 1.1))
                        improved = True
                        break

        # Update incumbent.
        if M < best_M - ACCEPT_TOL:
            best_M = M
            best_a = a.copy()
        else:
            stall += 1
            if not improved:
                kkt_step = max(0.05, kkt_step * 0.85)
                lin_step = max(0.02, lin_step * 0.85)
                recip_eta = max(0.05, recip_eta * 0.85)

            if stall > 900:
                noise_scale = 5.0e-5 / max(1.0, np.sqrt(float(n)))
                a = positive_normalize(best_a + rng.normal(0.0, noise_scale, n))
                kkt_step = 0.18
                lin_step = 0.05
                recip_eta = 0.18
                stall = 0

        it += 1

    # Final long polish with a few tight active sets.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 5.0:
        cbest = conv_full_fft(best_a, N)
        Mb = float(np.max(cbest))
        if not np.isfinite(Mb) or Mb > best_M + 1e-12:
            Mb = best_M

        try:
            alpha = threshold_lag_weights(cbest, 0.0015)
            B = rfft(alpha, N)
            cand, Mc = kkt_rollout(best_a, B, 0.30, 80, Mb, N)
            if Mc < best_M - ACCEPT_TOL:
                best_a, best_M = cand, float(Mc)
        except Exception:
            pass

        try:
            cbest2 = conv_full_fft(best_a, N)
            alpha = softmax_lag_weights(cbest2, 90.0)
            B = rfft(alpha, N)
            cand, Mc = kkt_rollout(best_a, B, 0.22, 80, best_M, N)
            if Mc < best_M - ACCEPT_TOL:
                best_a, best_M = cand, float(Mc)
        except Exception:
            pass

    return best_a, best_M


def get_parent_sequence():
    """Read the globally provided previous construction if available."""
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if arr.size >= 16:
            arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
            arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
            s = float(arr.sum())
            if s > 0:
                return arr / s
    except Exception:
        pass
    return None


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2385.0):
    """
    Returns the best sequence found before the deadline.
    """
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    budget_s = float(budget_s)
    deadline = t0 + budget_s - 15.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    parent = get_parent_sequence()

    if parent is None or len(parent) < 32:
        n = 8192
        a0 = analytic_init(n)
    else:
        a0 = positive_normalize(parent)
        n = len(a0)
        if n > 16384:
            old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
            new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, 16384, endpoint=False)
            res = np.interp(new, old, a0, left=float(a0[0]), right=float(a0[-1]))
            a0 = positive_normalize(res)
            n = len(a0)

    N = next_fft_size(n)
    M0 = conv_max_fft(a0, N)
    best_obj = 2.0 * float(n) * float(M0)
    best_a = a0.copy()
    best_n = n

    print("Initial: n=%d max=%g objective=%.8f" % (best_n, M0, best_obj), flush=True)

    # Phase 1: polish the parent/main length.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    phase1_time = min(0.70 * budget_s, max(0.0, remaining - 60.0))
    if phase1_time > 30.0:
        print("Phase 1: main KKT polish, %.0f sec" % phase1_time, flush=True)
        a1, M1 = optimize_kkt(best_a, phase1_time, seed=seed + 1, verbose=True)
        obj1 = 2.0 * float(len(a1)) * float(M1)
        if np.isfinite(obj1) and obj1 < best_obj:
            best_obj = float(obj1)
            best_a = a1
            best_n = len(a1)
            print("  improved objective: %.8f" % best_obj, flush=True)

    # Phase 2: try a doubled length if it is reasonable.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 300.0 and best_n * 2 <= 16384:
        big_n = int(best_n) * 2
        a_big = upsample_sequence(best_a, 2)
        phase2_time = min(0.22 * budget_s, max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 60.0))
        if phase2_time > 120.0:
            print("Phase 2: length %d KKT polish, %.0f sec" % (len(a_big), phase2_time), flush=True)
            a2, M2 = optimize_kkt(a_big, phase2_time, seed=seed + 2, verbose=True)
            obj2 = 2.0 * float(len(a2)) * float(M2)
            if np.isfinite(obj2) and obj2 < best_obj:
                best_obj = float(obj2)
                best_a = a2
                best_n = len(a2)
                print("  improved objective: %.8f" % best_obj, flush=True)

    # Phase 3: perturbed / mixed restart from current best.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 120.0:
        b = best_a
        choice = int(rng.integers(0, 4))
        ncur = len(b)
        if choice == 0:
            noise_scale = 5.0e-5 / max(1.0, np.sqrt(float(ncur)))
            a3 = positive_normalize(b + rng.normal(0.0, noise_scale, ncur))
        elif choice == 1:
            mult = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.03, ncur))
            a3 = positive_normalize(b * mult)
        elif choice == 2:
            an = analytic_init(ncur)
            a3 = positive_normalize(0.75 * b + 0.25 * an)
        else:
            an = analytic_init(ncur)
            a3 = positive_normalize(0.1 * b + 0.9 * an)

        phase3_time = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 25.0)
        if phase3_time > 60.0:
            print("Phase 3: perturbed restart, %.0f sec" % phase3_time, flush=True)
            a3, M3 = optimize_kkt(a3, phase3_time, seed=seed + 3, verbose=False)
            obj3 = 2.0 * float(len(a3)) * float(M3)
            if np.isfinite(obj3) and obj3 < best_obj:
                best_obj = float(obj3)
                best_a = a3
                best_n = len(a3)
                print("  improved objective: %.8f" % best_obj, flush=True)

    # Final normalization and sanity.
    final = positive_normalize(best_a)
    final = np.clip(np.asarray(final, dtype=np.float64), 0.0, 1000.0)
    final_list = [float(x) for x in final]

    Nfinal = next_fft_size(len(final_list))
    Mfinal = conv_max_fft(np.asarray(final_list, dtype=np.float64), Nfinal)
    objfinal = 2.0 * float(len(final_list)) * float(Mfinal)
    print("Final selected: n=%d max=%.12g objective=%.8f" %
          (len(final_list), Mfinal, objfinal), flush=True)

    # If time remains, verify with the official evaluator when reasonably fast.
    if len(final_list) <= 20000 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 5.0:
        try:
            ver = evaluate_sequence(final_list)
            if np.isfinite(ver):
                print("Official verification: %.8f" % float(ver), flush=True)
                if float(ver) < objfinal:
                    objfinal = float(ver)
        except Exception:
            pass

    return final_list
```
